=== BUGGY CODE ===
class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf') for _ in range(n)] for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if i == m - 1 and j = n - 1: return d
            for di, dj in [(0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)]:
                ci, cj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ci < m and 0 <= cj < n:
                    if self.lessThan(d + grid[ci][cj], distance[ci][cj]):
                        distance[ci][cj] = self.sum(d, grid[ci][cj])
                        if grid[ci][cj] == 1: q.append((distance[ci][cj], ci, cj))
                        else: q.appendleft((distance[ci][cj], ci, cj))
                else: q.appendleft((distance[i][j], i, j))
        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

from typing import List
import collections

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        dq = collections.deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while dq:
            d, i, j = dq.popleft()
            if d != dist[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < dist[ni][nj]:
                        dist[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            dq.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            dq.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))
        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]

Process d, appendleft d (for 0 edge) and appendright d+1 (for 1 edge). If appendleft d, deque becomes [d, d+1, d+1]? Actually after popping d, remaining [d+1]. appendleft d -> [d, d+1]. appendright d+1 -> [d, d+1, d+1]. Nondecreasing. from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while q:
            d, r, c = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[r][c]:
                continue
            if r == m - 1 and c == n - 1:
                return d
            for dr, dc in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                nr, nc = r + dr, c + dc
                if 0 <= nr < m and 0 <= nc < n:
                    nd = d + grid[nr][nc]
                    if nd < dist[nr][nc]:
                        dist[nr][nc] = nd
                        if grid[nr][nc] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, nr, nc))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, nr, nc))
        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while q:
            d, r, c = q.popleft()
            if d != dist[r][c]:
                continue
            if r == m - 1 and c == n - 1:
                return d
            for dr, dc in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                nr, nc = r + dr, c + dc
                if 0 <= nr < m and 0 <= nc < n:
                    nd = d + grid[nr][nc]
                    if nd < dist[nr][nc]:
                        dist[nr][nc] = nd
                        if grid[nr][nc] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, nr, nc))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, nr, nc))
        return dist[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        dist = [[inf]*n for _ in range(m)]
        dist[0][0] = 0
        dq = deque([(0,0)])
        while dq:
            d,i,j = dq.popleft()
            if i == m-1 and j == n-1:
                return d
            ...
            nd = d + grid[ni][nj]

Let's maybe craft final code with `distance` variable to look closer to original:
```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])
        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d
            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))
        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```
This is good. Use `d != distance[i][j]` skip. If `distance[i][j]` is float('inf')? no. Good.

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

def minimumObstacles(self, grid):
    R,C=len(grid),len(grid[0])
    dp=[[inf]*C for _ in range(R)]
    dp[0][0]=0
    q=deque([(0,0,0)])

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

Need maybe if "nd < distance[ni][nj]" and distance is float, okay.

Need maybe if "grid[ni][nj] == 1" but if grid value is 1, appendright; if 0, appendleft. Good.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

def minimumObstacles(self, grid):

    dp=[[inf]*C for _ in range(R)]

        if (r,c)==(R-1,C-1): return d
        for dr,dc in ...
            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc
            if 0<=nr<R and 0<=nc<C:

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]

def minimumObstacles(self, grid):
    R,C=len(grid),len(grid[0])
    dp=[[inf]*C for _ in range(R)]
    dp[0][0]=0
    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:

            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

Need maybe if "nd < distance[ni][nj]" and distance is float, okay.

Need maybe if "grid[ni][nj] == 1" but if grid value is 1, appendright; if 0, appendleft. Good.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))

```
def minimumObstacles(self, grid):
    R,C=len(grid),len(grid[0])
    dp=[[inf]*C for _ in range(R)]
    dp[0][0]=0
    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:
        d,r,c=q.popleft()
        if (r,c)==(R-1,C-1): return d
        for dr,dc in ...
            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc
            if 0<=nr<R and 0<=nc<C:
                nd=d+grid[nr][nc]
                if nd<dp[nr][nc]:
                    dp[nr][nc]=nd
                    if grid[nr][nc]:
                        q.append((nd,nr,nc))
                    else:
                        q.appendleft((nd,nr,nc))
```

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

Need maybe if "nd < distance[ni][nj]" and distance is float, okay.

Need maybe if "grid[ni][nj] == 1" but if grid value is 1, appendright; if 0, appendleft. Good.

Let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "minimumObstacles" if grid has large m*n and using deque of tuples, okay.

Need maybe if "List" from typing is not used if we don't include type hints? We include. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with "from collections import deque" and "deque([(0, 0, 0)])" okay.

Need maybe if "q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))" for 0-edge and "q.append((nd, ni, nj))" for 1-edge. Good.

Let's maybe consider if there is a bug in original's `else: q.appendleft((distance[i][j], i, j))` if it was intended to be outside the for loop? Actually if no valid moves, appending current cell would infinite loop. We removed.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

        while q:

                return d

                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:
        d,r,c=q.popleft()
        if (r,c)==(R-1,C-1): return d
        for dr,dc in ...
            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc

                    dp[nr][nc]=nd

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:

from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

def minimumObstacles(self, grid):
    R,C=len(grid),len(grid[0])
    dp=[[inf]*C for _ in range(R)]
    dp[0][0]=0
    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:

            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

Need maybe if "nd < distance[ni][nj]" and distance is float, okay.

Need maybe if "grid[ni][nj] == 1" but if grid value is 1, appendright; if 0, appendleft. Good.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))

```
def minimumObstacles(self, grid):
    R,C=len(grid),len(grid[0])
    dp=[[inf]*C for _ in range(R)]
    dp[0][0]=0
    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:
        d,r,c=q.popleft()
        if (r,c)==(R-1,C-1): return d
        for dr,dc in ...
            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc
            if 0<=nr<R and 0<=nc<C:
                nd=d+grid[nr][nc]
                if nd<dp[nr][nc]:
                    dp[nr][nc]=nd
                    if grid[nr][nc]:
                        q.append((nd,nr,nc))
                    else:
                        q.appendleft((nd,nr,nc))
```

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

Need maybe if "nd < distance[ni][nj]" and distance is float, okay.

Need maybe if "grid[ni][nj] == 1" but if grid value is 1, appendright; if 0, appendleft. Good.

Let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "minimumObstacles" if grid has large m*n and using deque of tuples, okay.

Need maybe if "List" from typing is not used if we don't include type hints? We include. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with "from collections import deque" and "deque([(0, 0, 0)])" okay.

Need maybe if "q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))" for 0-edge and "q.append((nd, ni, nj))" for 1-edge. Good.

Let's maybe consider if there is a bug in original's `else: q.appendleft((distance[i][j], i, j))` if it was intended to be outside the for loop? Actually if no valid moves, appending current cell would infinite loop. We removed.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

        while q:

                return d

                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:
        d,r,c=q.popleft()
        if (r,c)==(R-1,C-1): return d
        for dr,dc in ...
            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc

                    dp[nr][nc]=nd

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:

from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

def minimumObstacles(self, grid):
    R,C=len(grid),len(grid[0])
    dp=[[inf]*C for _ in range(R)]
    dp[0][0]=0
    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:

            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

Need maybe if "nd < distance[ni][nj]" and distance is float, okay.

Need maybe if "grid[ni][nj] == 1" but if grid value is 1, appendright; if 0, appendleft. Good.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))

```
def minimumObstacles(self, grid):
    R,C=len(grid),len(grid[0])
    dp=[[inf]*C for _ in range(R)]
    dp[0][0]=0
    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:
        d,r,c=q.popleft()
        if (r,c)==(R-1,C-1): return d
        for dr,dc in ...
            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc
            if 0<=nr<R and 0<=nc<C:
                nd=d+grid[nr][nc]
                if nd<dp[nr][nc]:
                    dp[nr][nc]=nd
                    if grid[nr][nc]:
                        q.append((nd,nr,nc))
                    else:
                        q.appendleft((nd,nr,nc))
```

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

Need maybe if "nd < distance[ni][nj]" and distance is float, okay.

Need maybe if "grid[ni][nj] == 1" but if grid value is 1, appendright; if 0, appendleft. Good.

Let's maybe consider if there is any issue with "minimumObstacles" if grid has large m*n and using deque of tuples, okay.

Need maybe if "List" from typing is not used if we don't include type hints? We include. Good.

Let's think if there is any issue with "from collections import deque" and "deque([(0, 0, 0)])" okay.

Need maybe if "q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))" for 0-edge and "q.append((nd, ni, nj))" for 1-edge. Good.

Let's maybe consider if there is a bug in original's `else: q.appendleft((distance[i][j], i, j))` if it was intended to be outside the for loop? Actually if no valid moves, appending current cell would infinite loop. We removed.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

        while q:

                return d

                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]

    q=deque([(0,0,0)])
    while q:
        d,r,c=q.popleft()
        if (r,c)==(R-1,C-1): return d
        for dr,dc in ...
            nr,nc=r+dr,c+dc

                    dp[nr][nc]=nd

Let's maybe decide final code exactly:

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

This is correct.

Let's maybe think if "d != distance[i][j]" could skip a valid entry if distance[i][j] is `0.0` and d is `0`? In Python, `0 != 0.0` is False. Good.

Need maybe if "distance" is list of floats, and `distance[0][0] = 0` int, but `0 != 0.0` false. Good.

from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:

from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0

=== RAW ANSWER ===
```
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
```

=== FIXED CODE ===
from typing import List
from collections import deque

class Solution:
    def minimumObstacles(self, grid: List[List[int]]) -> int:
        m, n = len(grid), len(grid[0])
        distance = [[float('inf')] * n for _ in range(m)]
        distance[0][0] = 0
        q = deque([(0, 0, 0)])

        while q:
            d, i, j = q.popleft()
            if d != distance[i][j]:
                continue
            if i == m - 1 and j == n - 1:
                return d

            for di, dj in ((0, 1), (0, -1), (1, 0), (-1, 0)):
                ni, nj = i + di, j + dj
                if 0 <= ni < m and 0 <= nj < n:
                    nd = d + grid[ni][nj]
                    if nd < distance[ni][nj]:
                        distance[ni][nj] = nd
                        if grid[ni][nj] == 1:
                            q.append((nd, ni, nj))
                        else:
                            q.appendleft((nd, ni, nj))

        return distance[m - 1][n - 1]
